Proofs of the main results

Proof of Proposition 1.

  1. For each cell \(k=1,\ldots,d\), set \[p_k=P(X=k);\] when \(d=0\), all sums below are empty. When \(p_k>0\), let \(\pi_k=P(A=1\mid X=k)\). The finite-cell law carries an arm-cell outcome law \(\nu_{ak}\) for every arm \(a\in\{0,1\}\) and every cell, including cells with \(p_k=0\), and its totalized mean is \[\mu_{ak}:=\int y\,d\nu_{ak}(y).\] For every supported cell and every Borel set \(\mathfrak A\subseteq\mathbb R\), the observed arm-cell factorization is \[P_O(X=k,A=a,Y\in\mathfrak A) = p_k \begin{cases} \pi_k, & a=1,\\ 1-\pi_k, & a=0 \end{cases} \nu_{ak}(\mathfrak A).\] If \(p_k>0\), Assumption 3 and \(\epsilon>0\) make the displayed scalar positive for both arms. Hence, on supported cells, \(\nu_{ak}\) is the observed conditional law of \(Y\) given \((A=a,X=k)\), and \(\mu_{ak}\) is the corresponding observed conditional mean. If \(p_k=0\), then \(P_O(X=k)=0\); the totalized law \(\nu_{ak}\) and mean \(\mu_{ak}\) enter the finite formula below only through terms multiplied by \(p_k\).

  2. Fix \(a\in\{0,1\}\) and a supported cell \(k\), so \(p_k>0\). By Assumption 1, on \(\{X=k,A=a\}\), \[Y(a)=Y \quad\text{almost surely}.\] Together with the observed arm-cell factorization, this gives, for every Borel \(\mathfrak A\), \[P(X=k,A=a,Y(a)\in\mathfrak A) = p_k \begin{cases} \pi_k, & a=1,\\ 1-\pi_k, & a=0 \end{cases} \nu_{ak}(\mathfrak A).\] Applying Assumption 2 in cell \(k\) to the potential-outcome event \(\{Y(a)\in\mathfrak A\}\) yields \[P(X=k,A=a,Y(a)\in\mathfrak A)\,P(X=k) = P(X=k,A=a)\,P(X=k,Y(a)\in\mathfrak A).\] Here \(P(X=k)=p_k\), and \(P(X=k,A=a)\) is \(p_k\pi_k\) when \(a=1\) and \(p_k(1-\pi_k)\) when \(a=0\). The same overlap argument makes this arm-cell probability positive, so cancellation gives \[P(X=k,Y(a)\in\mathfrak A)=p_k\nu_{ak}(\mathfrak A).\] Equivalently, \[\mathcal L_P(Y(a);\,X=k)=p_k\,\nu_{ak},\] where the left side is the pushforward of the full-data law restricted to \(\{X=k\}\).

  3. The preceding restricted-law identity supplies the full-data integrability. On supported cells, Assumption 6 gives integrability of \(y\mapsto (y-\mu_{ak})^2\) under \(\nu_{ak}\). Thus \(y-\mu_{ak}\) is square-integrable, hence integrable, and adding the finite constant \(\mu_{ak}\) gives first-moment integrability of \(y\mapsto y\) under \(\nu_{ak}\). The restricted-law identity transfers this integrability to \(Y(a)\) on each supported cell. If \(p_k=0\), the full-data mass of \(\{X=k\}\) is \(0\), so the corresponding restricted measure is the zero measure. Since there are finitely many cells, \[Y(1)\in L^1(P),\qquad Y(0)\in L^1(P).\]

  4. For every \(a\in\{0,1\}\) and every cell \(k\), \[\int_{\{X=k\}}Y(a)\,dP = p_k\int y\,d\nu_{ak}(y) = p_k\mu_{ak}.\] On supported cells this is the integral form of the restricted-law identity in the second step and the defining integral for \(\mu_{ak}\). On zero-mass cells, the restricted measure is zero and \(p_k=0\), so both sides vanish.

  5. Apply the finite-fiber decomposition of the integral to the measurable finite-valued map \(X\). Using the integrability just established, \[\int Y(1)\,dP = \sum_{k=1}^{d}\int_{\{X=k\}}Y(1)\,dP, \qquad \int Y(0)\,dP = \sum_{k=1}^{d}\int_{\{X=k\}}Y(0)\,dP.\] Therefore \[\begin{aligned} \int\{Y(1)-Y(0)\}\,dP &= \sum_{k=1}^{d} \left\{ \int_{\{X=k\}}Y(1)\,dP - \int_{\{X=k\}}Y(0)\,dP \right\}\\ &= \sum_{k=1}^{d}p_k(\mu_{1k}-\mu_{0k}). \end{aligned}\] By Definition 4, \[\tau(P)=\sum_{k=1}^{d}p_k(\mu_{1k}-\mu_{0k}),\] so \[\tau(P) = \int\{Y(1)-Y(0)\}\,dP.\] On supported cells, the first step identifies \(\mu_{ak}\) with \(E_P[Y\mid A=a,X=k]\); cells with \(p_k=0\) contribute \(0\) to the totalized sum. Thus, when conditional expectations on zero-mass arm-cell events are chosen to equal the totalized means \(\mu_{ak}\), the same equality is written as \[\tau(P) = \int\{Y(1)-Y(0)\}\,dP = \sum_{k=1}^{d}p_k \left\{ E_P[Y\mid A=1,X=k]-E_P[Y\mid A=0,X=k] \right\}.\] The observed margin determines this finite-cell formula: \(p_k=P_O(X=k)\), and on every supported cell Assumption 3 gives positive observed mass in both arms, so the observed conditional laws determine the two arm-cell means. Zero-mass cells have weight \(p_k=0\).

∎
Proof of Lemma 1.

Fix \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M}^{\mathrm{unr}}\). Since \(M\ge1\), we have \(M\ge0\). For every supported cell \(k\), \[|\tau_k(P)| = |\mu_{1k}(P)-\mu_{0k}(P)| \le |\mu_{1k}(P)|+|\mu_{0k}(P)| \le M/2+M/2 =M .\]

For every cell \(k\), the product term in the all-cell formula of Definition 4 satisfies \[|p_k(P)\tau_k(P)|\le p_k(P)M .\] Indeed, the cell-mass range gives either \(p_k(P)=0\) or \(p_k(P)>0\). In the first case both sides are zero. In the second case, \[|p_k(P)\tau_k(P)| =p_k(P)|\tau_k(P)| \le p_k(P)M,\] using the supported-cell bound above and \(p_k(P)>0\). The finite cell masses satisfy \[\sum_{k\in[d]}p_k(P)\le1 .\]

Hence, using the all-cell formula of Definition 4, \[|\tau(P)| = \left|\sum_{k\in[d]}p_k(P)\tau_k(P)\right| \le \sum_{k\in[d]}|p_k(P)\tau_k(P)| \le \sum_{k\in[d]}p_k(P)M = \left(\sum_{k\in[d]}p_k(P)\right)M \le M .\]

Therefore, for every supported cell \(k\), \[|\delta_k(P)| = |\tau_k(P)-\tau(P)| \le |\tau_k(P)|+|\tau(P)| \le M+M = 2M .\]

The radius-two membership follows by keeping the same law and the same consistency, exchangeability, overlap, mean-normalization, and second-moment witnesses, using the numerical facts \(0\le2\le2\), and adding the just-proved supported-cell bound \[|\delta_k(P)|\le 2M=2\cdot M \qquad (p_k(P)>0)\] as the approximate-homogeneity witness. Thus every law in \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M}^{\mathrm{unr}}\) is represented in \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,2}\). Conversely, a member of \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,2}\) carries all fields required for \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M}^{\mathrm{unr}}\); forgetting the homogeneity and radius fields leaves the same law.

∎
Proof of Theorem 7.

  1. Fix an overlap parameter \(\epsilon\) with \(0<\epsilon<1/2\). By Theorem 1, choose \(C_{\epsilon}^{0}>0\) and a polynomial handle \((N_{\epsilon},\rho_{\epsilon})\), with \(\rho_{\epsilon}>0\), such that \[N_{\epsilon}\le n, \qquad d\le \rho_{\epsilon}n\log(en) \quad\Longrightarrow\quad 2\le K_n\] for all positive integers \(n,d\). The same construction gives, for every \(n,d\ge1\), every \(M\ge1\), and every \(0\le\sigma\le2\), measurable \([-M,M]\)-valued estimators \(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}\) and \(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}\), and for every \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\), \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \left(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}-\tau(P)\right)^2 \right] \le C_{\epsilon}^{0}M^2 \left\{ \frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right) \right\}\] and \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \left(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}-\tau(P)\right)^2 \right] \le C_{\epsilon}^{0}M^2 \left\{ \frac1n+\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2} \right\}.\] Set \[C_{\epsilon}:=\max\{1,C_{\epsilon}^{0}\}.\] Then \(C_{\epsilon}>0\) and \(C_{\epsilon}\ge C_{\epsilon}^{0}\), so both displayed risk bounds hold with \(C_{\epsilon}\) in place of \(C_{\epsilon}^{0}\).

  2. For fixed admissible \(n,d,M,\sigma\), put \[u_{n,d}:=\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}, \qquad h_{n,d,\sigma}:=\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2}, \qquad r_{n,d,\sigma}:=\frac1n+\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma}\}\}.\] The known-radius selector of Algorithm 3 is the three-branch estimator \[\widehat\tau_n^{\star} = \begin{cases} T_n^{\mathrm{col}}, & h_{n,d,\sigma}\le \min\{1,u_{n,d}\},\\ T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}, & h_{n,d,\sigma}> \min\{1,u_{n,d}\} \text{ and } u_{n,d}<\min\{1,h_{n,d,\sigma}\},\\ 0, & h_{n,d,\sigma}> \min\{1,u_{n,d}\} \text{ and } u_{n,d}\ge \min\{1,h_{n,d,\sigma}\}. \end{cases}\] The first two branches are elements of \(\mathcal T_{n,M}\) by the construction above. The zero branch is measurable and takes values in \([-M,M]\) because \(M\ge1\). Hence \(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}\in\mathcal T_{n,M}\).

  3. Fix \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\). On the collision branch, \(h_{n,d,\sigma}\le \min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\), so \[\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma}\}\}=h_{n,d,\sigma}.\] Therefore \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \left(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}-\tau(P)\right)^2 \right] \le C_{\epsilon}M^2\left(\frac1n+h_{n,d,\sigma}\right) = C_{\epsilon}M^2 r_{n,d,\sigma}.\] On the polynomial branch, \(u_{n,d}<\min\{1,h_{n,d,\sigma}\}\), so \[\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}=u_{n,d}, \qquad \min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma}\}\}=u_{n,d}.\] Thus \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \left(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}-\tau(P)\right)^2 \right] \le C_{\epsilon}M^2\left(\frac1n+u_{n,d}\right) = C_{\epsilon}M^2 r_{n,d,\sigma}.\] It remains to consider the zero branch. There \[h_{n,d,\sigma}> \min\{1,u_{n,d}\}, \qquad u_{n,d}\ge \min\{1,h_{n,d,\sigma}\}.\] These inequalities imply \(1\le u_{n,d}\): if \(u_{n,d}<1\), then \(h_{n,d,\sigma}>u_{n,d}\), while \(\min\{1,h_{n,d,\sigma}\}\le u_{n,d}\); if \(h_{n,d,\sigma}\le1\) this gives \(h_{n,d,\sigma}\le u_{n,d}<h_{n,d,\sigma}\), and if \(h_{n,d,\sigma}>1\) it gives \(1\le u_{n,d}<1\). Hence \(1\le u_{n,d}\). The first zero-branch inequality then gives \(1<h_{n,d,\sigma}\), so \(1\le h_{n,d,\sigma}\), and \[\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma}\}\}=1.\] Viewing the same law as a member of the unrestricted-radius class, the scale bound in Lemma 1 gives \[|\tau(P)|\le M.\] Since the selected estimator is identically zero on this branch, \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \left(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}-\tau(P)\right)^2 \right] = \tau(P)^2 \le M^2 \le C_{\epsilon}M^2\left(\frac1n+1\right) = C_{\epsilon}M^2 r_{n,d,\sigma},\] where the penultimate inequality uses \(C_{\epsilon}\ge1\) and \(n\ge1\). Together the three branches prove the asserted risk bound for every \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\).

  4. Put \(c_{\epsilon}:=1\). Then \(c_{\epsilon}>0\). The preceding all-alphabet bound applies to every positive \(n,d\), so it applies in particular under the restricted-dimensional hypothesis \[d\le c_{\epsilon}\frac{n^2}{\log(en)}.\] The constants \(C_{\epsilon},c_{\epsilon}\) and the same polynomial handle \((N_{\epsilon},\rho_{\epsilon})\) therefore satisfy all claims of the theorem.

∎
Proof of Proposition 5.

  1. First record the logarithmic estimate used below. If \(n\ge1\), then \[L_n=\log(en)=1+\log n .\] The standard bound \(\log n\le n^{1/2}/(1/2)=2\sqrt n\), together with \(\log n\ge0\) and \(\sqrt n\ge1\), gives \[0\le L_n\le 1+2\sqrt n\le 3\sqrt n .\] Squaring yields \[L_n^2\le 9n .\]

  2. We prove the all-alphabet comparison that supplies the constants. For \(n,d\ge1\), put \[\eta_{n,d}:=\frac{d}{n^2}, \qquad u_{n,d}:=\frac{d^2}{n^2L_n^2}.\] Since \(L_n\ge1\), both \(\eta_{n,d}\) and \(u_{n,d}\) are nonnegative. At radius two the collision term is \[2^2+\eta_{n,d}=4+\eta_{n,d}\ge1,\] and therefore \[\frac1n+\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},4+\eta_{n,d}\}\} = \frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}.\] At radius zero, \[\frac1n+\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},\eta_{n,d}\}\} \le \frac1n+\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\},\] and the capped converse expression at radius zero is exactly the right-hand side.

    It remains to compare the radius-two converse term \[\frac1n+\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}+4\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\] with \[\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}.\] If \(L_n^2\le d\), then \[\eta_{n,d}=\frac d{n^2}\le \frac{d^2}{n^2L_n^2}=u_{n,d},\] so \[\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},\eta_{n,d}\}\}=\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}\] and \[\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}\le \min\{1,u_{n,d}\}.\] Consequently \[\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\} \le \frac1n+\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}+4\min\{1,u_{n,d}\} \le 5\left(\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\right).\] If \(d<L_n^2\), the logarithmic estimate from the previous step gives \[d\le 9n, \qquad \eta_{n,d}\le \frac9n .\] Hence \[\frac1n+\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}\le \frac{10}{n}.\] The upper-frontier term at radius zero is at least \(1/n\), so \[\frac1{10}\left(\frac1n+\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}\right) \le \frac1n+\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},\eta_{n,d}\}\}.\] The remaining upper bound at radius zero follows from the displayed monotonicity inequality. For radius two, nonnegativity gives the lower bound \[\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\} \le \frac1n+\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}+4\min\{1,u_{n,d}\},\] while \(\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}\le 9/n\) gives \[\frac1n+\min\{1,\eta_{n,d}\}+4\min\{1,u_{n,d}\} \le 10\left(\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\right).\] Thus, uniformly over all \(n,d\ge1\), the all-alphabet comparisons hold with \[c=\frac1{10}, \qquad C=10 .\] The radius-two equality of attainable laws is exactly the consequence of Lemma 1.

  3. Now fix \(\epsilon\in(0,1/2)\) and choose \[c_\epsilon=1, \qquad c=\frac1{10}, \qquad C=10 .\] Then \(0<c_\epsilon\le1\) and \(0<c\le C\). Suppose \(n,d\ge1\) and \[d\le c_\epsilon\frac{n^2}{L_n}=\frac{n^2}{L_n}.\] Because \(L_n\ge1\), \[d\le n^2, \qquad \frac d{n^2}\le1, \qquad \min\left\{1,\frac d{n^2}\right\}=\frac d{n^2}.\] Applying the all-alphabet comparison from the previous step and replacing the capped term by \(d/n^2\) gives \[c\left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right) \le \frac1n+\min\left\{1,\min\left\{u_{n,d},\frac d{n^2}\right\}\right\} \le C\left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right),\] and \[c\left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right) \le \frac1n+\min\left\{1,\frac d{n^2}\right\} \le C\left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right).\] The class identity is the radius-two conclusion of Lemma 1. Finally, the all-alphabet radius-two calculation gives, for every \(n,d\ge1\), \[\frac1n+\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},4+d/n^2\}\} = \frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\},\] and its converse comparison gives \[c\left(\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\right) \le \frac1n+\min\left\{1,\frac d{n^2}\right\}+4\min\{1,u_{n,d}\} \le C\left(\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\right).\]

∎
Proof of Theorem 8.

  1. Fix an overlap parameter \(\epsilon\) with \(0<\epsilon<1/2\). Apply Theorem 3 to obtain constants \(\bar c_{\epsilon}\) and \(\bar b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}\) such that \[0<\bar c_{\epsilon}\le 1, \qquad 0<\bar b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}},\] and, for every admissible \(n,d,M,\sigma\), \[\bar c_{\epsilon}M^2 \left\{ \frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right) +\sigma^2 \min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right) \right\} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\] Set \(c_{\epsilon}:=\bar c_{\epsilon}\) and \(b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}:=\bar b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}\).

  2. Now fix \(n,d,M,\sigma\) satisfying the hypotheses of the present statement, and put \[L:=\log(en).\] Since \(0<n\), we have \(n\ge1\), hence \[L=\log(e n)=1+\log n\ge1.\] The restricted range and \(c_{\epsilon}\le1\) give \[d\le c_{\epsilon}\frac{n^2}{L} \le c_{\epsilon}n^2 \le n^2 .\] Because \(n>0\), division by \(n^2\) yields \[\frac d{n^2}\le1, \qquad\text{so}\qquad \min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right)=\frac d{n^2}.\] Substituting this identity into the all-alphabet bound displayed above gives \[\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma} \ge c_{\epsilon}M^2 \left\{ \frac1n+\frac d{n^2} +\sigma^2 \min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right) \right\}.\]

  3. The radial certificate supplied by Theorem 3 uses the same constants \(c_{\epsilon}\) and \(b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}\). Unpacking the certificate, there are an integer cap \(d_{\mathrm{rad}}\), a binary control-zero source class \(\mathcal S_{\mathrm{rad}}\), a padding map into the \(d\)-alphabet, an embedding \(\Psi\) that sends each source member into \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\), and binary full-observation couplings such that the channel acts by \[B'(a)\mid B(a) \sim \operatorname{Bernoulli}\!\left\{ \frac12+\frac{\sigma}{2}\left(B(a)-\frac12\right) \right\}, \qquad Y(a):=M\{B'(a)-1/2\}.\] The source target in this certificate is the total treated-arm weighted functional \[\psi(P) := \sum_{k=1}^{d_{\mathrm{rad}}} p_k(P)\,\mu_{1k}^{\mathrm{bin}}(P),\] where \(\mu_{1k}^{\mathrm{bin}}(P)\) is the treated-arm binary mean coordinate carried by the source law in cell \(k\), defined for every source cell including zero-mass cells. Thus, for all \(P_0,P_1\in\mathcal S_{\mathrm{rad}}\), the same certificate gives \[\tau(\Psi(P_1))-\tau(\Psi(P_0)) = M\frac{\sigma}{2} \left\{ \psi(P_1)-\psi(P_0) \right\}.\] It also gives the estimator-wise lower bound: for every \(\widehat\tau\in\mathcal T_{n,M}\), the class of total measurable \([-M,M]\)-valued estimators from Definition 5, there is \(P\in\mathcal S_{\mathrm{rad}}\) such that \[c_{\epsilon}M^2\sigma^2 \min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right) \le \mathbb E_{\Psi(P)} \left[ \{\widehat\tau-\tau(\Psi(P))\}^2 \right].\]

∎
Proof of Theorem 6.

Fix an overlap parameter \(\epsilon\) with \(0<\epsilon<1/2\).

  1. First form the all-alphabet package. By Lemma 13, there are \(C_{\mathrm{poly},\epsilon}>0\) and a polynomial handle \[\mathfrak h_\epsilon=(N_{\mathrm{cal},\epsilon},\rho_{\mathrm{cal},\epsilon}), \qquad \rho_{\mathrm{cal},\epsilon}>0,\] such that, for all integers \(n,d\ge1\), \[N_{\mathrm{cal},\epsilon}\le n,\qquad d\le \rho_{\mathrm{cal},\epsilon}n\log(en) \quad\Longrightarrow\quad 2\le K_n,\] and, for every \(M\ge1\), \(0\le\sigma\le2\), and \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\), \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \left(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}-\tau(P)\right)^2 \right] \le C_{\mathrm{poly},\epsilon}M^2 \left\{ \frac1n+ \min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right) \right\}.\] The same cited construction gives measurability of \(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}\) and \[T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}(s)\in[-M,M] \qquad\text{for every }s\in\mathcal O_d^n .\]

    By Lemma 12, there is \(C_{\mathrm{col},\epsilon}>0\) such that, for every \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), \(0\le\sigma\le2\), and \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\), \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \left(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}-\tau(P)\right)^2 \right] \le C_{\mathrm{col},\epsilon}M^2 \left( \frac1n+\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2} \right).\] For the admissibility of the collision estimator, write its unclipped inner statistic as the total sample function \[\Gamma_{\mathrm{col}}(s) := \begin{cases} \displaystyle \frac{1}{R_n(s)} \sum_{k=1}^d N_k(s)I_k(s) \left[ \mathbf 1\{N_{1k}(s)>0\}\frac{S_{1k}(s)}{N_{1k}(s)\vee 1} - \mathbf 1\{N_{0k}(s)>0\}\frac{S_{0k}(s)}{N_{0k}(s)\vee 1} \right], & R_n(s)>0,\\[1.2em] 0,& R_n(s)=0. \end{cases}\] Here \(m\vee1\) denotes \(\max\{m,1\}\). The displayed arm factors are total functions of the sample and agree with the arm-cell means of Algorithm 2 on positive arm-cell counts; multiplication by \(I_k\) selects exactly the cells where both arm-cell counts are positive. Hence Algorithm 2 gives \[T_n^{\mathrm{col}}(s)=\max\{-M,\min(M,\Gamma_{\mathrm{col}}(s))\}.\] The counts, indicators, outcome totals, finite sums, totalized ratios, and the fallback at \(R_n=0\) are measurable functions of the sample, so \(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}\) is measurable. Since \(M\ge1\), clipping gives \[T_n^{\mathrm{col}}(s)\in[-M,M] \qquad\text{for every }s\in\mathcal O_d^n .\]

    Set \[C_\epsilon:=\max\{C_{\mathrm{poly},\epsilon}, C_{\mathrm{col},\epsilon}\}.\] Then \(C_\epsilon>0\). The two displayed rate factors are nonnegative for \(n,d\ge1\) and \(0\le\sigma\le2\), and \(M^2\ge0\); hence replacing \(C_{\mathrm{poly},\epsilon}\) and \(C_{\mathrm{col},\epsilon}\) by \(C_\epsilon\) preserves the two upper bounds.

  2. Define \[c_\epsilon:=1.\] Then \(c_\epsilon>0\). For arbitrary \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\), the all-alphabet package from the first step gives the measurability and range assertions for both estimators. If \[d\le c_\epsilon\frac{n^2}{\log(en)},\] then for every \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\) the same all-alphabet risk bounds apply under this range condition: \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \left(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}-\tau(P)\right)^2 \right] \le C_\epsilon M^2 \left\{ \frac1n+ \min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right) \right\},\] and \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \left(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}-\tau(P)\right)^2 \right] \le C_\epsilon M^2 \left( \frac1n+\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2} \right).\] Together with the active-range property of \(\mathfrak h_\epsilon\), these are the asserted constants and calibrated handle.

∎
Proof of Theorem 10.

  1. Fix \(0<\epsilon<1/2\), and apply Theorem 5 at this \(\epsilon\). Choose the range constant \[c_\epsilon:=1 .\] Then \(c_\epsilon>0\). The triangular benchmark algebra, the exact algebraic elbow, the residual wedge implication, and the diagonal witness assertions in the present statement are exactly the corresponding conclusions supplied by Theorem 5, with the same definitions of \(L_n\), \(b_{n,d}\), \(u_{n,d}\), \(q_{n,d,\sigma}\), \(\ell_{n,d,\sigma}\), and \(r_{n,d,\sigma}\). In particular, the denominator in the residual-wedge ratio is the capped converse benchmark \[\frac1t+\min\left\{1,\frac{d_t}{t^2}\right\} +\sigma_t^2\min\{1,u_{t,d_t}\},\] so the all-alphabet residual-wedge implication gives precisely \[\frac{b_{t,d_t}}{u_{t,d_t}}\to0,\qquad u_{t,d_t}\to0,\qquad \frac{b_{t,d_t}}{\sigma_t^2}\to0,\qquad \sigma_t^2\to0 .\] For the witness \(d_n=n\), \(\sigma_n=L_n^{-1/2}\), the same all-alphabet conclusion gives membership in the residual wedge and the two displayed comparisons \[\frac{q_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}}}{\ell_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}}}\asymp L_n, \qquad \frac{q_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}}}{\ell_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}}}\to\infty .\]

  2. It remains only to specialize the fixed-interior and matching-elbow minimax clauses. Let \(\underline\sigma\in(0,2]\). The fixed-radius clause of Theorem 5 supplies constants \(c,C\) with \(0<c\le C\) such that, for all \(n,d\in\mathbb N\), \(M,\sigma\in\mathbb R\), \[n>0,\qquad d>0,\qquad M\ge1,\qquad 0\le\sigma,\qquad \underline\sigma\le\sigma,\qquad \sigma\le2\] imply \[cM^2r_{n,d,\sigma} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma} \le CM^2r_{n,d,\sigma}.\] Since the implication holds for every positive \(d\), it also holds under the additional displayed range condition \[d\le c_\epsilon\frac{n^2}{L_n} =\frac{n^2}{L_n}.\] Thus the fixed-interior-radii conclusion follows with the same constants \(c,C\).

  3. Now fix \(K\ge0\). The matching-elbow clause of Theorem 5 supplies constants \(c,C\) with \(0<c\le C\) such that, whenever \[n>0,\qquad d>0,\qquad M\ge1,\qquad 0\le\sigma\le2\] and at least one of \[u_{n,d}\ge1,\qquad u_{n,d}\le Kb_{n,d},\qquad \sigma^2\le Kb_{n,d}\] holds, one has \[cM^2r_{n,d,\sigma} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma} \le CM^2r_{n,d,\sigma}.\] This is exactly the matching-elbows assertion in the present theorem. Combining this with the clauses recorded in the first step and the choice \(c_\epsilon=1\) proves all asserted conclusions.

∎
Proof of Theorem 9.

  1. Fix an overlap parameter \(\epsilon\) with \(0<\epsilon<1/2\). By Theorem 4, choose constants \(c_{\epsilon}\) and \(C_{\epsilon}\) such that \[0<c_{\epsilon}\le 1,\qquad 1\le C_{\epsilon},\qquad c_{\epsilon}\le C_{\epsilon},\] and such that, for every \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\), \[c_{\epsilon}M^2 \left\{ \frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac{d}{n^2}\right) +\sigma^2\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right) \right\} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\] and \[\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma} \le C_{\epsilon}M^2 \left\{ \frac1n+ \min\left(1,\min\left(\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)},\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2}\right)\right) \right\}.\] These constants already have the normalization required in the statement.

  2. Now fix \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\), and assume \[d\le \frac{c_{\epsilon}n^2}{\log(en)} .\] Since \(n\ge1\), \[\log(en)=\log(e)+\log n=1+\log n\ge1.\] The numerator \(c_{\epsilon}n^2\) is nonnegative, so \[d\le \frac{c_{\epsilon}n^2}{\log(en)} \le c_{\epsilon}n^2 \le n^2,\] where the last inequality uses \(c_{\epsilon}\le1\). Dividing by \(n^2>0\) gives \[\frac{d}{n^2}\le1, \qquad\text{and hence}\qquad \min\left(1,\frac{d}{n^2}\right)=\frac{d}{n^2}.\] Substituting this identity into the lower bound from the preceding step yields \[c_{\epsilon}M^2\left\{ \frac{1}{n}+\frac{d}{n^2} +\sigma^2\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right) \right\} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\] The upper bound is exactly the upper bound supplied in the preceding step. Therefore the displayed lower and upper bounds hold throughout the stated restricted range.

∎
Proof of Lemma 2.

Fix \(0<\epsilon<1/2\). Let \(a_\epsilon,b_\epsilon>0\) and \(N_\epsilon\in\mathbb N\) be the constants supplied by Lemma 4. Let \(c_{\mathrm{par}}>0\) be the constant in Lemma 14. Set \[\bar b_\epsilon:=\min\{b_\epsilon,1/2\}, \qquad K_\epsilon:=1+N_\epsilon+\frac1{\bar b_\epsilon}, \qquad c_\epsilon:=\min\left\{\frac{a_\epsilon \bar b_\epsilon}{4}, \frac{c_{\mathrm{par}}}{K_\epsilon}\right\}.\] Then \(\bar b_\epsilon>0\), \(K_\epsilon>0\), and \(c_\epsilon>0\).

Fix \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\). Put \[x:=\bar b_\epsilon n^2 .\] First suppose that \[N_\epsilon\le n, \qquad 1\le x .\] Define the capped source alphabet size \[d_0:=\min\{d,\lfloor x\rfloor\}.\] Then \(d_0\ge1\), \(d_0\le d\), and \[d_0\le b_\epsilon n^2 .\] The exact binary source bound gives \[a_\epsilon\left(\frac1n+\frac{d_0}{n^2}\right) \le \mathsf R^{\mathrm{bin}}_{n,d_0,\epsilon},\] where \(\mathsf R^{\mathrm{bin}}_{n,d_0,\epsilon}\) denotes the minimax risk over the uniform-mass exactly homogeneous binary source experiment. Since \(a_\epsilon>0\) and \(n,d_0\ge1\), the source lower-risk level \[L_{\mathrm{ex}}:=\frac{a_\epsilon}{2} \left(\frac1n+\frac{d_0}{n^2}\right)\] is strictly below \(\mathsf R^{\mathrm{bin}}_{n,d_0,\epsilon}\). Hence, for every measurable binary estimator there is a source law \(P\) such that \[\frac{a_\epsilon}{2} \left(\frac1n+\frac{d_0}{n^2}\right) \le E_P\left[ \left\{\widehat\psi-\psi(P)\right\}^2 \right].\]

Embed this source law into the \(d\)-cell real-outcome experiment by zero-mass padding and by the affine outcome map \[Y(a)=M\{B(a)-1/2\},\qquad a\in\{0,1\}.\] The padding keeps the source cells inside the first \(d_0\) target cells and assigns zero mass to cells \(d_0+1,\ldots,d\). The affine map gives conditional means in \([-M/2,M/2]\), conditional second central moments bounded by \(M^2\), and exact homogeneity; with the assumed bounds on \(\epsilon\), \(M\), and \(\sigma\), the embedded law belongs to \[\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\] Write \(\Phi_M(P)\) for this embedded real law. Its target satisfies \[\tau(\Phi_M(P))=M\psi(P),\] and the observed-data law is the pushforward of the binary observed-data law under the padded affine sample map. Thus every \(T\in\mathcal T_{n,M}\) from Definition 5 induces a measurable binary estimator by applying \(M^{-1}T\) after that sample map, and \[M^2\frac{a_\epsilon}{2} \left(\frac1n+\frac{d_0}{n^2}\right) \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\]

It remains in this branch to compare the capped source rate with the desired all-\(d\) rate. Since \(1\le x\), \[\frac{x}{2}\le \lfloor x\rfloor .\] If \(d\le\lfloor x\rfloor\), then \(d_0=d\) and \(d/n^2\le \bar b_\epsilon\le1\). If \(d>\lfloor x\rfloor\), then \(d_0=\lfloor x\rfloor\) and \(d_0/n^2\ge \bar b_\epsilon/2\). In both cases, \[\frac{\bar b_\epsilon}{2} \left\{\frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right)\right\} \le \frac1n+\frac{d_0}{n^2}.\] Since \(c_\epsilon\le a_\epsilon \bar b_\epsilon/4\), \[c_\epsilon M^2 \left\{\frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right)\right\} \le M^2\frac{a_\epsilon}{2} \left(\frac1n+\frac{d_0}{n^2}\right) \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\]

On the complementary branch, either \(n<N_\epsilon\) or \(\bar b_\epsilon n^2<1\). In the first case, \(N_\epsilon/n\ge1\); in the second, \(\bar b_\epsilon n\le1\), so \((1/\bar b_\epsilon)/n\ge1\). Thus, in either case, \[\frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right) \le \frac{K_\epsilon}{n}.\] Using \(c_\epsilon\le c_{\mathrm{par}}/K_\epsilon\) and Lemma 14, \[c_\epsilon M^2 \left\{\frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right)\right\} \le \frac{c_{\mathrm{par}}M^2}{n} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\]

Combining the two cases proves the stated bound for all \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\).

∎
Proof of Theorem 1.

Fix an overlap parameter \(0<\epsilon<1/2\).

  1. By Lemma 13, choose \(C_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{poly}}>0\) and a polynomial handle \(h_{\epsilon}=(N_{\epsilon},\rho_{\epsilon})\). The handle has \(\rho_{\epsilon}>0\) and satisfies the calibration clause \[N_{\epsilon}\le n,\qquad d\le \rho_{\epsilon}n\log(en) \quad\Longrightarrow\quad 2\le K_n\] for all integers \(n,d\ge1\). The same lemma gives, for every \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), \(0\le\sigma\le2\), and \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\), that the estimator \(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}\) indexed by this handle is measurable, takes values in \([-M,M]\), and satisfies \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \{T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}-\tau(P)\}^2 \right] \le C_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{poly}}M^2 \left\{ \frac1n+\min\!\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right) \right\}.\]

  2. By Lemma 12, choose \(C_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{col}}>0\) such that, for every \(n,d\ge1\) and every \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\), \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \{T_n^{\mathrm{col}}-\tau(P)\}^2 \right] \le C_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{col}}M^2 \left( \frac1n+\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2} \right).\] Here the bounds \(M\ge1\) and \(0\le\sigma\le2\) are the corresponding model-class fields in \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\) from Definition 1.

  3. The collision estimator also has the required admissibility property. For a sample \(s\), Algorithm 2 defines \[T_n^{\mathrm{col}}(s) = \operatorname{clip}_{[-M,M]} \left( \begin{cases} \displaystyle \frac{ \sum_{k=1}^d \mathbf 1\{N_{0k}(s)>0,\,N_{1k}(s)>0\} N_k(s) \left( \overline Y_{1k}(s)-\overline Y_{0k}(s) \right) }{R_n(s)},&R_n(s)>0,\\[2ex] 0,&R_n(s)=0. \end{cases} \right),\] where each empirical arm-cell mean is the indicator-totalized ratio \(S_{ak}(s)/N_{ak}(s)\) on \(N_{ak}(s)>0\) and is assigned value \(0\) when \(N_{ak}(s)=0\). The counts are finite sums of sample indicators, the totals are finite sums of indicator-weighted outcomes, and the branches use measurable comparisons, arithmetic operations, and a finite sum over cells. Thus the inner statistic is a measurable function of the \(n\)-sample. Since \(M\ge1\) gives \(0\le M\), clipping to \([-M,M]\) is measurable and has range contained in \([-M,M]\). Therefore \(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}\) is a measurable map of the sample and satisfies \[T_n^{\mathrm{col}}(s)\in[-M,M] \qquad\text{for every sample }s.\]

  4. Define \[C_{\epsilon}:=\max\{C_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{poly}}, C_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{col}}\}.\] Then \(C_{\epsilon}>0\). For \(n,d\ge1\) and \(0\le\sigma\le2\), \[0\le \frac1n+\min\!\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right), \qquad 0\le \frac1n+\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2}.\] Together with \(M^2\ge0\), the inequalities \(C_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{poly}}\le C_{\epsilon}\) and \(C_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{col}}\le C_{\epsilon}\) upgrade the two pointwise risk inequalities above to the same inequalities with \(C_{\epsilon}\). With \(h=h_{\epsilon}\), the calibration, measurability, range, and pointwise risk clauses hold simultaneously for every \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), \(0\le\sigma\le2\), and every \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\) from Definition 1. Since the resulting right-hand sides do not depend on \(P\), taking the supremum over \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\) gives the two displayed supremum inequalities.

∎
Proof of Theorem 2.

Fix \(0<\epsilon<1/2\).

Step 1. Common calibration and envelopes. By Theorem 1, choose a polynomial handle \((N_{\epsilon},\rho_{\epsilon})\), with \(\rho_{\epsilon}>0\), and a constant \(\Gamma_{\epsilon}>0\). Put \[C_{\epsilon}:=\max\{1,\Gamma_{\epsilon}\}.\] Then \(C_{\epsilon}>0\), \(\Gamma_{\epsilon}\le C_{\epsilon}\), and \(1\le C_{\epsilon}\). The selected handle carries the displayed calibration implication for \(K_n\): for all integers \(n,d\ge1\), \[N_{\epsilon}\le n, \qquad d\le \rho_{\epsilon}n\log(en) \quad\Longrightarrow\quad 2\le K_n .\] For fixed integers \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\), define \[u_{n,d}:=\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}, \qquad h_{n,d,\sigma}:=\sigma^2+\frac{d}{n^2}.\] The construction also gives measurable \([-M,M]\)-valued estimators \(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}\) and \(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}\), and for every \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\), \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \left(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}-\tau(P)\right)^2 \right] \le \Gamma_{\epsilon}M^2 \left(\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\right)\] and \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \left(T_n^{\mathrm{col}}-\tau(P)\right)^2 \right] \le \Gamma_{\epsilon}M^2 \left(\frac1n+h_{n,d,\sigma}\right).\]

Step 2. The selector is admissible. The known-radius rule in Algorithm 3 is \[\widehat\tau_n^{\star} = \begin{cases} T_n^{\mathrm{col}},& h_{n,d,\sigma}\le \min\{1,u_{n,d}\},\\ T_n^{\mathrm{poly}},& u_{n,d}<\min\{1,h_{n,d,\sigma}\},\\ 0,&\text{otherwise}. \end{cases}\] The first two branches are measurable and take values in \([-M,M]\) by Theorem 1. The zero branch is measurable and also takes values in \([-M,M]\) because \(M\ge1\). Hence \(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}\) is measurable and \(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}(s)\in[-M,M]\) for every sample \(s\).

Step 3. Collision branch. Assume \[h_{n,d,\sigma}\le \min\{1,u_{n,d}\}.\] Then \(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}=T_n^{\mathrm{col}}\), and \[\min\{1,\min(u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma})\}=h_{n,d,\sigma}.\] Therefore, for every \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\), \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \left(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}-\tau(P)\right)^2 \right] \le \Gamma_{\epsilon}M^2 \left(\frac1n+h_{n,d,\sigma}\right) \le C_{\epsilon}M^2 \left(\frac1n+\min\{1,\min(u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma})\}\right),\] where the last inequality uses \(\Gamma_{\epsilon}\le C_{\epsilon}\) and the nonnegativity of \(M^2(1/n+h_{n,d,\sigma})\).

Step 4. Polynomial branch. Assume the collision-branch condition fails and \[u_{n,d}<\min\{1,h_{n,d,\sigma}\}.\] Then \(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}=T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}\), \[\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}=u_{n,d}, \qquad \min\{1,\min(u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma})\}=u_{n,d}.\] Hence, for every \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\), \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \left(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}-\tau(P)\right)^2 \right] \le \Gamma_{\epsilon}M^2 \left(\frac1n+u_{n,d}\right) \le C_{\epsilon}M^2 \left(\frac1n+\min\{1,\min(u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma})\}\right),\] again because \(\Gamma_{\epsilon}\le C_{\epsilon}\) and the rate factor is nonnegative.

Step 5. Zero branch. It remains to consider the branch in which \[h_{n,d,\sigma}>\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}, \qquad u_{n,d}\ge \min\{1,h_{n,d,\sigma}\}.\] These two inequalities imply \(1\le u_{n,d}\): if \(u_{n,d}<1\), then the first inequality gives \(h_{n,d,\sigma}>u_{n,d}\), while the second gives \(\min\{1,h_{n,d,\sigma}\}\le u_{n,d}\), a contradiction whether \(h_{n,d,\sigma}\le1\) or \(h_{n,d,\sigma}>1\). With \(1\le u_{n,d}\), the first displayed inequality gives \(1\le h_{n,d,\sigma}\), and consequently \[\min\{1,\min(u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma})\}=1.\] On this branch \(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}=0\). Viewing the law of \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\) as a member of the unrestricted class obtained by forgetting the radius condition, Lemma 1 gives \[|\tau(P)|\le M.\] Thus \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \left(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}-\tau(P)\right)^2 \right] = \tau(P)^2 \le M^2.\] Since \(C_{\epsilon}\ge1\), \(M^2\ge0\), and \(n\ge1\), \[M^2 \le C_{\epsilon}M^2\left(\frac1n+1\right) = C_{\epsilon}M^2 \left(\frac1n+\min\{1,\min(u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma})\}\right).\]

Combining the three branches yields, for every \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\), \[E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \left(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}-\tau(P)\right)^2 \right] \le C_{\epsilon}M^2 \left(\frac1n+\min\{1,\min(u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma})\}\right) = C_{\epsilon}M^2 r_{n,d,\sigma}.\] Together with the measurability and range conclusion in Step 2, this proves the stated selector guarantee.

∎
Proof of Proposition 3.

Take \(c=1/10\) and \(C=10\). Fix integers \(n,d\ge1\), and write \[\Lambda_n:=\log(en),\qquad u_{n,d}:=\frac{d^2}{n^2\Lambda_n^2},\qquad v_{n,d}:=\frac d{n^2}.\] Also set \[\theta^{\mathrm{ex}}_{n,d}:=\frac1n+\min(1,v_{n,d}), \qquad \theta^{\mathrm{poly}}_{n,d}:=\frac1n+\min(1,u_{n,d}).\] The quantities \(1/n\), \(u_{n,d}\), and \(v_{n,d}\) are nonnegative.

  1. Since \(n\ge1\), \[\Lambda_n =\log(en)=1+\log n\ge1,\] and hence \(\Lambda_n^2>0\). At radius two the collision term is \[2^2+v_{n,d}=4+v_{n,d}\ge1.\] Therefore \[\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d},4+v_{n,d}\} =\frac1n+\min(1,u_{n,d}) =\theta^{\mathrm{poly}}_{n,d}.\] This proves the endpoint identity, and also gives the two-sided comparison of the radius-two upper frontier expression with \(\theta^{\mathrm{poly}}_{n,d}\), because \(\theta^{\mathrm{poly}}_{n,d}\ge0\) and \(1/10\le1\le10\).

  2. We use the elementary logarithmic estimate \[\Lambda_n^2\le 9n.\] Indeed, \(\log n\le 2\sqrt n\), while \(\sqrt n\ge1\) and \(\log n\ge0\). Thus \[\Lambda_n=1+\log n\le 1+2\sqrt n\le3\sqrt n,\] and squaring gives \(\Lambda_n^2\le9n\).

  3. First consider the case \(\Lambda_n^2\le d\). Since \(d>0\), \[v_{n,d}=\frac d{n^2} \le \frac{d^2}{n^2\Lambda_n^2} =u_{n,d}.\] Consequently \[\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d},v_{n,d}\} = \frac1n+\min(1,v_{n,d}) = \theta^{\mathrm{ex}}_{n,d}.\] The radius-zero converse expression is also exactly \[\frac1n+\min(1,v_{n,d})=\theta^{\mathrm{ex}}_{n,d}.\] For the radius-two converse expression, \[\frac1n+\min(1,v_{n,d})+4\min(1,u_{n,d}) \ge \theta^{\mathrm{poly}}_{n,d},\] and, using \(\min(1,v_{n,d})\le\min(1,u_{n,d})\), \[\frac1n+\min(1,v_{n,d})+4\min(1,u_{n,d}) \le \frac1n+5\min(1,u_{n,d}) \le 5\theta^{\mathrm{poly}}_{n,d}.\] All required comparisons in this case follow from \(1/10\le1\le5\le10\).

  4. Now consider the case \(d<\Lambda_n^2\). By the logarithmic estimate, \[d\le9n, \qquad\text{so}\qquad v_{n,d}\le\frac9n.\] The radius-zero upper expression satisfies \[\frac1n \le \frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d},v_{n,d}\} \le \frac1n+\min(1,v_{n,d}) = \theta^{\mathrm{ex}}_{n,d},\] and \[\theta^{\mathrm{ex}}_{n,d} =\frac1n+\min(1,v_{n,d}) \le \frac1n+\frac9n = \frac{10}{n}.\] Hence \[\frac1{10}\theta^{\mathrm{ex}}_{n,d} \le \frac1n \le \frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d},v_{n,d}\} \le \theta^{\mathrm{ex}}_{n,d} \le 10\theta^{\mathrm{ex}}_{n,d}.\] The radius-zero converse expression again equals \(\theta^{\mathrm{ex}}_{n,d}\). For the radius-two converse expression, \[\frac1n+\min(1,v_{n,d})+4\min(1,u_{n,d}) \ge \theta^{\mathrm{poly}}_{n,d},\] and \[\frac1n+\min(1,v_{n,d})+4\min(1,u_{n,d}) \le \frac{10}{n}+4\min(1,u_{n,d}) \le 10\theta^{\mathrm{poly}}_{n,d}.\] Together with the radius-two identity from the first step, this proves the stated two-sided comparisons in the second case.

  5. It remains to identify the two radius-two classes. By Lemma 1, for every \(d\), every \(\epsilon\in\mathbb R\), and every \(M\in\mathbb R\), each law in \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M}^{\mathrm{unr}}\) is represented by some member of \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,2}\) with the same law, and conversely each law in \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,2}\) is represented by some member of \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M}^{\mathrm{unr}}\) with the same law. Thus the represented laws are exactly the same at radius two.

∎
Proof of Theorem 5.

Let \[\omega_{n,d,\sigma} := \frac1n+\min\left\{1,\frac d{n^2}\right\} +\sigma^2\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}.\] Thus \(\omega_{n,d,\sigma}\) is the capped converse benchmark appearing in the residual-ratio display. We use the all-alphabet two-sided bracket \[c_0M^2\omega_{n,d,\sigma} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma} \le C_0M^2r_{n,d,\sigma},\] valid for \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\), as supplied by Theorem 4.

  1. Suppose \(L_n^2<d<nL_n\), and put \[\eta_{n,d}:=\frac d{n^2}, \qquad \zeta_\sigma:=\sigma^2 .\] Then \(n\ge1\), \(L_n>0\), and \(d>0\). The two strict inequalities give \[\eta_{n,d}\le u_{n,d}, \qquad u_{n,d}<1.\] Hence \[q_{n,d,\sigma} = \frac1n+\min\{u_{n,d},\zeta_\sigma+\eta_{n,d}\}.\] For nonnegative \(\eta_{n,d},u_{n,d},\zeta_\sigma\) with \(\eta_{n,d}\le u_{n,d}\), \[\frac12\left\{ \frac1n+\eta_{n,d}+\min(u_{n,d},\zeta_\sigma) \right\} \le \frac1n+\min\{u_{n,d},\zeta_\sigma+\eta_{n,d}\} \le \frac1n+\eta_{n,d}+\min(u_{n,d},\zeta_\sigma).\] The upper bound follows from the two bounds by \(u_{n,d}\) and by \(\zeta_\sigma+\eta_{n,d}\). For the lower bound, if \(u_{n,d}\le\zeta_\sigma+\eta_{n,d}\), the right-hand minimum is \(u_{n,d}\) and \(\eta_{n,d}\le u_{n,d}\); otherwise the right-hand minimum is \(\zeta_\sigma+\eta_{n,d}\), and then \(\min(u_{n,d},\zeta_\sigma)=\zeta_\sigma\). Substituting \(b_{n,d}=n^{-1}+\eta_{n,d}\) gives the triangular display. The identity \[\ell_{n,d,\sigma}=b_{n,d}+\sigma^2u_{n,d}\] is the definition of \(\ell_{n,d,\sigma}\).

  2. Fix \(\underline\sigma\in(0,2]\) and define \[\Gamma_{\underline\sigma}:=\max\{1,\underline\sigma^{-2}\}.\] If \(\underline\sigma\le\sigma\le2\), then \[r_{n,d,\sigma} \le \frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\} \le \Gamma_{\underline\sigma} \left[ \frac1n+\sigma^2\min\{1,u_{n,d}\} \right] \le \Gamma_{\underline\sigma}\omega_{n,d,\sigma}.\] Combining this comparison with the two-sided bracket gives \[\frac{c_0}{\Gamma_{\underline\sigma}}M^2r_{n,d,\sigma} \le c_0M^2\omega_{n,d,\sigma} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma} \le C_0M^2r_{n,d,\sigma}.\] Thus the fixed-interior-radius assertion holds with \(c=c_0/\Gamma_{\underline\sigma}\) and \(C=C_0\).

  3. Fix \(K\ge0\). We prove \[r_{n,d,\sigma}\le(1+K)\omega_{n,d,\sigma}\] under each matching-elbow alternative. If \(1\le u_{n,d}\), then \[r_{n,d,\sigma} \le \frac1n+\min\left\{1,\sigma^2+\frac d{n^2}\right\}.\] When \(d/n^2\le1\), the condition \(1\le u_{n,d}\) gives \[\omega_{n,d,\sigma} = \frac1n+\frac d{n^2}+\sigma^2,\] and the preceding display is bounded by \(\omega_{n,d,\sigma}\). When \(d/n^2>1\), the same condition gives \[\omega_{n,d,\sigma} = \frac1n+1+\sigma^2,\] and the preceding display is again bounded by \(\omega_{n,d,\sigma}\). Thus \(r_{n,d,\sigma}\le\omega_{n,d,\sigma}\) in the first matching-elbow case.

    If \(u_{n,d}\le Kb_{n,d}\) and \(d/n^2\le1\), then \[r_{n,d,\sigma} \le \frac1n+u_{n,d} \le (1+K)b_{n,d} \le (1+K)\omega_{n,d,\sigma};\] if \(d/n^2>1\), then \[r_{n,d,\sigma}\le\frac1n+1\le\omega_{n,d,\sigma} \le(1+K)\omega_{n,d,\sigma}.\] If \(\sigma^2\le Kb_{n,d}\) and \(d/n^2\le1\), then \[r_{n,d,\sigma} \le b_{n,d}+\sigma^2 \le (1+K)b_{n,d} \le (1+K)\omega_{n,d,\sigma};\] the case \(d/n^2>1\) is again covered by \(r_{n,d,\sigma}\le n^{-1}+1\le\omega_{n,d,\sigma}\). The two-sided bracket therefore yields \[\frac{c_0}{1+K}M^2r_{n,d,\sigma} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma} \le C_0M^2r_{n,d,\sigma}.\]

  4. For \(n\ge1\), \(L_n>0\). Multiplying the inequality \(u_{n,d}\le b_{n,d}\) by \(n^2L_n^2\) gives \[u_{n,d}\le b_{n,d} \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad d^2 \le \left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right)n^2L_n^2 = nL_n^2+dL_n^2.\]

  5. If \(d\le\sqrt n\,L_n\), then \(d^2\le nL_n^2\), and hence \(d^2\le nL_n^2+dL_n^2\). If \(d\le L_n^2\), then \(d^2\le dL_n^2\le nL_n^2+dL_n^2\). The exact algebraic equivalence above gives \(u_{n,d}\le b_{n,d}\) in both cases.

  6. Let \(d_n\) and \(\sigma_n\) satisfy the admissibility hypothesis and suppose \[\frac{r_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}}{\omega_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}}\to\infty .\] For every fixed \(K\ge0\), divergence gives an eventual lower bound \[1+K< \frac{r_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}}{\omega_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}}.\] Intersect this eventual set with admissibility and with \(n>0\); then \(d_n\ge1\), \(0\le\sigma_n\le2\), and \(\omega_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}>0\). If one of the matching-elbow alternatives \[1\le u_{n,d_n}, \qquad u_{n,d_n}\le Kb_{n,d_n}, \qquad \sigma_n^2\le Kb_{n,d_n}\] holds at such an index, the matching-elbow comparison above gives \[\frac{r_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}}{\omega_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}}\le1+K,\] contradicting the lower bound. Hence, for each fixed \(K\ge0\), all three alternatives are eventually false. Taking \(K=a^{-1}\) for any \(a>0\) gives \[\frac{b_{n,d_n}}{u_{n,d_n}}\to0, \qquad \frac{b_{n,d_n}}{\sigma_n^2}\to0.\] Next fix \(a>0\), set \(\rho_a=\sqrt a\), and set \(\Gamma_a=\max\{1,\rho_a^{-2}\}\). Divergence gives eventually \[\Gamma_a< \frac{r_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}}{\omega_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}}.\] At any sufficiently large index with \(\sigma_n^2\ge a\), admissibility gives \(\rho_a\le\sigma_n\), and the fixed-radius comparison above gives \[\frac{r_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}}{\omega_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}}\le\Gamma_a,\] a contradiction. Therefore \(\sigma_n^2\to0\).

    It remains to show \(u_{n,d_n}\to0\). The \(K=0\) avoidance gives \(u_{n,d_n}<1\) eventually. For a target \(a\ge1\) this already gives \(u_{n,d_n}<a\) eventually. For \(0<a<1\), take \(n\) so large that \[\frac{b_{n,d_n}}{u_{n,d_n}}<\frac12, \qquad \sigma_n^2<\frac a2, \qquad \frac{r_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}}{\omega_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}}>a^{-1}, \qquad u_{n,d_n}<1.\] If \(u_{n,d_n}\ge a\), then \[\frac{d_n}{n^2}\le b_{n,d_n}<\frac12u_{n,d_n}, \qquad \sigma_n^2<\frac12u_{n,d_n},\] so \(\sigma_n^2+d_n/n^2<u_{n,d_n}\) and \(d_n/n^2\le1\). Therefore \[r_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}=b_{n,d_n}+\sigma_n^2, \qquad \omega_{n,d_n,\sigma_n} = b_{n,d_n}+\sigma_n^2u_{n,d_n}.\] Since \(u_{n,d_n}\ge a\), \[\frac{r_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}}{\omega_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}} = \frac{b_{n,d_n}+\sigma_n^2} {b_{n,d_n}+\sigma_n^2u_{n,d_n}} \le a^{-1},\] contradicting the chosen lower bound. Thus \(u_{n,d_n}\to0\), and the residual-wedge implication is proved.

  7. For the diagonal sequence \(d_n=n\) and \(\sigma_n=L_n^{-1/2}\), eventually \(n\ge1\), \(L_n\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma_n\le1\le2\). Moreover \[b_{n,n}=\frac2n, \qquad u_{n,n}=\frac1{L_n^2}, \qquad \sigma_n^2=\frac1{L_n}.\] Since \(L_n\to\infty\) and \(L_n^k/n\to0\) for each fixed natural power \(k\), \[\frac{b_{n,n}}{u_{n,n}}=\frac{2L_n^2}{n}\to0, \qquad u_{n,n}\to0, \qquad \frac{b_{n,n}}{\sigma_n^2}=\frac{2L_n}{n}\to0, \qquad \sigma_n^2\to0.\] Thus the diagonal sequence lies in the residual wedge.

  8. Along the same diagonal, \[q_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}} = \frac1n+\frac1{L_n^2}, \qquad \ell_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}} = \frac2n+\frac1{L_n^3}.\] Because \(L_n^3/n\to0\), eventually \(L_n^3/n<1\). On that event, \[\frac13L_n \le \frac{q_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}}}{\ell_{n,n,L_n^{-1/2}}} \le 2L_n.\] Hence the ratio is eventually of order \(L_n\). Since \(L_n\to\infty\), the same ratio diverges to infinity.

∎
Proof of Theorem 4.

  1. Fix an overlap parameter \(\epsilon\) with \(0<\epsilon<1/2\). By Theorem 2, choose a constant \(C_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{up}}>0\) and a polynomial calibration handle such that, for every \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\), the corresponding known-radius selector \(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}\in\mathcal T_{n,M}\) satisfies \[\sup_{P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}} E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[ \{\widehat\tau_n^{\star}-\tau(P)\}^2 \right] \le C_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{up}}M^2 r_{n,d,\sigma}.\] By Theorem 3, choose \(c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{lo}}\) and \(b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}\) such that \[0<c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{lo}}\le1,\qquad 0<b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}},\] and, for every such \(n,d,M,\sigma\), \[c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{lo}}M^2\ell_{n,d,\sigma} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\] Set \[c_{\epsilon}:=c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{lo}}, \qquad C_{\epsilon}:=\max\{1,C_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{up}}\}.\] Then \[0<c_{\epsilon}\le1,\qquad 1\le C_{\epsilon},\qquad c_{\epsilon}\le C_{\epsilon}.\]

  2. Fix \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\). The lower bound is exactly the one supplied by Theorem 3 with the constants fixed above: \[c_{\epsilon}M^2\ell_{n,d,\sigma} = c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{lo}}M^2\ell_{n,d,\sigma} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\] The accompanying radius-channel certificate in Theorem 3 also supplies a realized source family and an embedding into the same model class. Applying its source-risk assertion to the zero source estimator gives a source law, and the embedding-membership part of the certificate gives an embedded law \(P_{\bullet}\) with \[P_{\bullet}\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\] Thus the model class is nonempty for these indices.

  3. For any estimator \(\widehat\eta\in\mathcal T_{n,M}\), define its worst-case risk over this class by \[\mathcal W_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}(\widehat\eta) := \sup_{P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}} E_{Q_P^{(n)}}\!\left[\{\widehat\eta-\tau(P)\}^2\right].\] We first record the lower-boundedness needed to apply the infimum in Definition 5. Fix \(\widehat\eta\in\mathcal T_{n,M}\). In the vacuous branch where the model index is empty, the real supremum convention gives \(\mathcal W_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}(\widehat\eta)=0\). In the nonvacuous branch, consider the range of mean-squared errors indexed by \(P\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\). If this range is bounded above, choose any law in the class; its mean-squared error is the integral of a square and is therefore nonnegative, and it is bounded by the supremum defining \(\mathcal W_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}(\widehat\eta)\). If the range is unbounded above, the supremum is in its unbounded-above case and the inequality \(0\le\mathcal W_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}(\widehat\eta)\) is immediate. Hence \[0\le\mathcal W_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}(\widehat\eta) \qquad\text{for every }\widehat\eta\in\mathcal T_{n,M}.\] The collection of worst-case risks is therefore bounded below by \(0\). Using Definition 5 and the admissibility of \(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}\), \[\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma} = \inf_{\widehat\eta\in\mathcal T_{n,M}} \mathcal W_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}(\widehat\eta) \le \mathcal W_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}).\]

  4. Applying the selector guarantee from Theorem 2 gives \[\mathcal W_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}(\widehat\tau_n^{\star}) \le C_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{up}}M^2 r_{n,d,\sigma}.\] Moreover, \[u_{n,d}\ge0,\qquad h_{n,d,\sigma}\ge0,\] so \[r_{n,d,\sigma} = \frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma}\} \ge \frac1n > 0.\] Therefore \(M^2r_{n,d,\sigma}\ge0\), and \(C_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{up}}\le C_{\epsilon}\) implies \[C_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{up}}M^2 r_{n,d,\sigma} \le C_{\epsilon}M^2 r_{n,d,\sigma}.\] Combining the previous displays yields \[\mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma} \le C_{\epsilon}M^2 r_{n,d,\sigma}.\] Together with the lower bound supplied by Theorem 3 and instantiated above, this proves the asserted bracket for all \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\).

∎
Proof of Proposition 4.

Fix \(\epsilon\in(0,1/2)\) and assume the published binary collision guarantee stated in the proposition. We prove the simultaneous conclusion in three steps.

  1. The assumed guarantee is exactly the first component of the simultaneous assertion: it supplies the stated constants \(C_\epsilon,b_\epsilon>0\) and the two displayed bias and variance bounds for every admissible \(n,d,P\), and \(\sigma_{\mathrm{bin}}\).

  2. For the pointwise algebraic comparison, fix \(n,d\in\mathbb N\) and \(\sigma\in\mathbb R\) in the stated range. The defining order property of the minimum gives \[\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma}\}\} \le \min\{u_{n,d},h_{n,d,\sigma}\} \le h_{n,d,\sigma}.\] This is the asserted pointwise inequality.

  3. For the asymptotic comparison, let \(d_n\) and \(\sigma_n\) satisfy the stated sequence hypotheses. Limits along \(\mathbb N\) are unchanged by discarding finitely many initial indices, so work on the cofinal set \(n>0\). There \(d_n>0\), and hence \[h_{n,d_n,\sigma_n} = \sigma_n^2+\frac{d_n}{n^2} > 0.\] Also \(u_{n,d_n}\ge0\), \(h_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}\ge0\), and \(1\ge0\). Therefore \[0 \le \frac{\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d_n},h_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}\}\}} {h_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}}.\] The same minimum inequalities, now using the left branch of the inner minimum, give \[\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d_n},h_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}\}\} \le \min\{u_{n,d_n},h_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}\} \le u_{n,d_n}.\] Dividing by the positive denominator \(h_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}\) yields, for all sufficiently large \(n\), \[0 \le \frac{\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d_n},h_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}\}\}} {h_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}} \le \frac{u_{n,d_n}}{h_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}}.\] By hypothesis the right-hand side converges to \(0\), so the squeeze theorem gives \[\frac{\min\{1,\min\{u_{n,d_n},h_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}\}\}} {h_{n,d_n,\sigma_n}} \to0 .\] This proves the sequence comparison and completes the proof.

∎
Proof of Proposition 2.

Fix \(0<\epsilon<1/2\).

  1. Choose the constants as follows. From Lemma 2 take a constant \(\bar c_{\mathrm{ex}}>0\). From Theorem 3 take constants \(\bar c_{\mathrm{rad}},\bar b_{\mathrm{rad}}>0\) and its radius-channel transfer certificate. From Lemma 13 take a constant \(\bar C_{\mathrm{up}}>0\) and a polynomial handle \((K_0,A)\), so that, for all \(n,d\ge1\), \[K_0\le n,\qquad d\le A n\log(en) \quad\Longrightarrow\quad 2\le K_n .\] Set \[\omega_\epsilon:=\min\left\{\frac12,\,8\left(\frac12-\epsilon\right)^2\right\}, \qquad \vartheta_\epsilon:=\frac14 \min\left\{\frac1{100},\,\frac{\omega_\epsilon^2}{64}\right\},\] and define \[c_{\mathrm{ex}}:=\min\{\bar c_{\mathrm{ex}},\vartheta_\epsilon,1\}, \qquad c_{\mathrm{rad}}:=\bar c_{\mathrm{rad}}, \qquad b_{\mathrm{rad}}:=\bar b_{\mathrm{rad}}, \qquad C_{\mathrm{up}}:=\bar C_{\mathrm{up}} .\] All four constants are positive, and \(c_{\mathrm{ex}}\le1\).

  2. Fix \(d\ge1\) and \(M\ge1\). For a binary observed record \(z=(x,a,b)\), define \[\varphi_M(z):=\bigl(x,a,M(b-\tfrac12)\bigr),\] where \(b\in\{0,1\}\) is read as a real number. Let \(\Phi_M(P)\) be the real-outcome law whose observed law is \[Q_{\Phi_M(P)}=(\varphi_M)_{\#}Q_P,\] whose cell masses and propensities agree with those of \(P\), and whose positive arm-cell conditional outcome laws satisfy \[\operatorname{Law}_{\Phi_M(P)}(Y\mid A=a,X=k) = \bigl(b\mapsto M(b-\tfrac12)\bigr)_{\#} \operatorname{Law}_{P}(B\mid A=a,X=k).\] Its full-data law is formed by sampling \((x,a,b)\) from the binary observed law, independently imputing the missing binary potential outcome from the opposite arm-cell Bernoulli law, writing the completed binary potentials as \((\tilde b_0,\tilde b_1)\) with \(\tilde b_a=b\), and applying the affine map \[(x,a,\tilde b_0,\tilde b_1) \longmapsto \bigl(x,a,M(\tilde b_0-\tfrac12),M(\tilde b_1-\tfrac12),M(\tilde b_a-\tfrac12)\bigr).\] This construction is an affine binary embedding. We use the totalized finite-cell convention for this construction: the displayed arm-cell mean is defined for every arm and cell, while the conditional-mean assumptions in Assumptions 4 and 6 use it only on positive-mass cells. With this convention, \[\mu_{ak}(\Phi_M(P)) = M\{\mu_{ak}^{\mathrm{bin}}(P)-\tfrac12\}\] for every arm and cell.

    If \(P\in\mathcal D_d^{\mathrm{bin}}(\epsilon)\), overlap is unchanged because the cell masses and propensities are unchanged. The displayed full-data construction is consistent, and the independent missing-potential augmentation gives the conditional-exchangeability identities. Also, on every positive-mass cell, \[|\mu_{ak}(\Phi_M(P))| = M\left|\mu_{ak}^{\mathrm{bin}}(P)-\frac12\right| \le \frac M2\] and the affine binary outcome satisfies \[E_{\Phi_M(P)}\!\left[ \{Y-\mu_{ak}(\Phi_M(P))\}^2\mid A=a,X=k \right]\le M^2 .\] These certificates put \(\Phi_M(P)\) in \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M}^{\mathrm{unr}}\).

    If \(P\in\mathcal H_d^{\mathrm{bin}}(\epsilon)\), then \[\tau_k(\Phi_M(P)) = M\{\mu_{1k}^{\mathrm{bin}}(P)-\mu_{0k}^{\mathrm{bin}}(P)\},\] and \[\tau(\Phi_M(P)) = M\sum_{k=1}^d p_k(P) \{\mu_{1k}^{\mathrm{bin}}(P)-\mu_{0k}^{\mathrm{bin}}(P)\}.\] The binary effects are common across cells, and the cell masses sum to one, so \[|\tau_k(\Phi_M(P))-\tau(\Phi_M(P))|=0\] on every positive-mass cell. Together with the preceding consistency, exchangeability, overlap, mean-normalization, and second-moment certificates, this gives \(\Phi_M(P)\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,0}\).

  3. For strictness, choose one cell \(k_\circ\). For each \(0\le\sigma\le2\), let \(\mathfrak W_\circ\) be the one-cell real-outcome law with all mass on \(k_\circ\), propensity \(1/2\), control outcome identically \(0\), and treated outcome distributed as the symmetric two-point law on \(\{-M/2,M/2\}\). Its arm means are both \(0\), so \[\tau_{k_\circ}(\mathfrak W_\circ)=\tau(\mathfrak W_\circ)=0, \qquad |\tau_{k_\circ}(\mathfrak W_\circ)-\tau(\mathfrak W_\circ)| =0\le\sigma M .\] The constructed full-data law is consistent and conditionally exchangeable. Overlap holds in the only positive-mass cell because its propensity is \(1/2\) and \(0<\epsilon<1/2\). Mean normalization and the second-central-moment bound hold from the endpoint scale \(M/2\), the zero arm means, and \(M\ge1\). Hence \[\mathfrak W_\circ\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\] For every affine binary image \(\Phi_M(P)\), the control arm-cell outcome law at \(k_\circ\) is supported on \(\{-M/2,M/2\}\). Since \(M\ge1\), it assigns mass \(0\) to \(\{0\}\). The law \(\mathfrak W_\circ\) assigns mass \(1\) to \(\{0\}\) in that same conditional law. Thus \[\Phi_M(P)\ne \mathfrak W_\circ\] for every binary law \(P\).

  4. Now fix \(n\ge1\), \(0\le\sigma\le2\), and assume \[d\le c_{\mathrm{ex}}n^2 .\] Since \(c_{\mathrm{ex}}\le1\), this implies \(d/n^2\le1\). Let \[\mathcal B_{n,d,\epsilon} := \inf_{\widehat\psi} \sup_{P\in\mathcal H_d^{\mathrm{bin}}(\epsilon)} E_{P^{\otimes n}}\!\left[ \{\widehat\psi-\psi(P)\}^2 \right]\] be the fixed-sample binary exact-homogeneity minimax risk. For the parametric term, take the null source law \(P_0\) with uniform cell masses, propensity \(1/2\), and arm-cell binary means \(1/2\) in both arms. Let \[\Delta_n:=\frac{2/5}{\sqrt n}, \qquad \Delta_n^2=\frac{4}{25n},\] and take \(P_1\) with the same cell masses, propensity, and control means, and treated arm-cell means \(1/2+\Delta_n\). Since \(n\ge1\), \(\Delta_n\le2/5\), so both endpoint laws belong to \(\mathcal H_d^{\mathrm{bin}}(\epsilon)\), with \[\psi(P_0)=0, \qquad \psi(P_1)=\Delta_n .\] Moreover \[n\left\{\frac{(1/2)\Delta_n^2}{(1/2)(1-1/2)}\right\} =\frac{8}{25} \le \log 2,\] and the product total-variation bound for this Bernoulli endpoint experiment gives \[\operatorname{TV}\!\bigl(P_0^{\otimes n},P_1^{\otimes n}\bigr)\le \frac12 .\] Le Cam’s two-point inequality therefore gives \[\frac{1}{100n} = \frac{\Delta_n^2}{16} \le \mathcal B_{n,d,\epsilon}.\] Also, by Lemma 3, \[\frac{\omega_\epsilon^2}{64}\frac{d}{n^2} \le \mathcal B_{n,d,\epsilon}.\] Therefore, with \(\alpha_\epsilon:=\min\{1/100,\omega_\epsilon^2/64\}\), \[c_{\mathrm{ex}}\left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right) \le \frac{\alpha_\epsilon}{4}\left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right) \le \frac12\,\mathcal B_{n,d,\epsilon} < \mathcal B_{n,d,\epsilon},\] where the strict final inequality uses the positive parametric endpoint lower bound. Hence, for every measurable source estimator, some \(P\in\mathcal H_d^{\mathrm{bin}}(\epsilon)\) attains source risk at least \(c_{\mathrm{ex}}(n^{-1}+d/n^2)\). Given a real-outcome estimator \(\widehat\tau\in\mathcal T_{n,M}\), define the source estimator \[\widehat\psi(s) := \frac1M\,\widehat\tau\bigl(\varphi_M(s_1),\ldots,\varphi_M(s_n)\bigr), \qquad s=(s_1,\ldots,s_n).\] The coordinatewise pushforward identity and the target-scaling identity give \[Q_{\Phi_M(P)}^{(n)} = (\varphi_M^{(n)})_{\#}P^{\otimes n}, \qquad \tau(\Phi_M(P))=M\psi(P).\] Consequently \[\operatorname{MSE}_{\Phi_M(P)}(\widehat\tau) = M^2 E_{P^{\otimes n}}\!\left[ \{\widehat\psi-\psi(P)\}^2 \right] \ge c_{\mathrm{ex}}M^2 \left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right).\] This proves the estimator-wise exact-subclass lower transfer.

  5. The minimax exact lower bound follows from Lemma 2. Since \(d/n^2\le1\) and \(c_{\mathrm{ex}}\le\bar c_{\mathrm{ex}}\), \[c_{\mathrm{ex}}M^2\left(\frac1n+\frac d{n^2}\right) \le \bar c_{\mathrm{ex}}M^2 \left(\frac1n+\min\left\{1,\frac d{n^2}\right\}\right) \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\] For the radius term, Theorem 3 gives both the transfer certificate with constants \(c_{\mathrm{rad}},b_{\mathrm{rad}}\) and \[c_{\mathrm{rad}}M^2 \left\{ \frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right) +\sigma^2\min(1,u_{n,d}) \right\} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\] Because all discarded summands are nonnegative, \[c_{\mathrm{rad}}M^2\sigma^2\min(1,u_{n,d}) \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\] This is the stated radius-channel transfer and certificate.

  6. Finally, Lemma 13 applied to the chosen handle gives, for every \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), \(0\le\sigma\le2\), and every \(Q\in\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\), a measurable estimator \(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}\) with \[T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}(s)\in[-M,M] \quad\text{for every sample }s,\] and \[\operatorname{MSE}_{Q}(T_n^{\mathrm{poly}}) \le C_{\mathrm{up}}M^2 \left(\frac1n+\min\{1,u_{n,d}\}\right).\] The active-range implication for \(K_n\) is the handle property recorded in the first step.

∎
Proof of Theorem 3.

Fix \(0<\epsilon<1/2\).

  1. Let \(a_{\epsilon}>0\), \(b_{\epsilon}>0\), and \(N_{\epsilon}\) be the constants from Lemma 5. Set \[b_{0,\epsilon}:=\min\{b_{\epsilon},4\}, \qquad b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}:=\frac{b_{0,\epsilon}}2, \qquad \bar b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}:=b_{\epsilon},\] \[a_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}:=a_{\epsilon}, \qquad c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}:=\frac{a_{\epsilon}b_{0,\epsilon}^{2}}{128}, \qquad N_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad},0}:= \max\left\{3,N_{\epsilon},\left\lceil\frac{2}{b_{0,\epsilon}}\right\rceil\right\}.\] Then \(a_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}>0\), \(b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}>0\), \(\bar b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}>b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}\), and \(c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}>0\). For every \(n,d\ge1\), define the capped radial alphabet \[d_{\mathrm{rad}} := \min\left\{ d,\max\left\{1,\left\lfloor b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}\,n\log(en)\right\rfloor\right\} \right\}.\] Then \(0<d_{\mathrm{rad}}\le d\). If, in addition, \(N_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad},0}\le n\), then \[d_{\mathrm{rad}} \le \bar b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}n\log n.\] A source law on \(d_{\mathrm{rad}}\) cells carries, for every arm \(a\) and every cell \(k\), an arm-cell response distribution, zero-mass and empty arm-cells included; write \(\mu_{ak}^{\mathrm{bin}}(P)\) for its mean, which agrees with \(E_P[B\mid A=a,X=k]\) whenever the arm-cell probability is positive. With that convention every summand below is defined on every cell, and zero-mass cells contribute nothing because they enter weighted by \(p_k(P)=0\). For a control-zero one-arm source law \(P\) on \(d_{\mathrm{rad}}\) cells, write \[\psi(P):= \sum_{k=1}^{d_{\mathrm{rad}}} p_k(P)\,\mu_{1k}^{\mathrm{bin}}(P).\] Under the same large-sample condition, every measurable uniformly bounded estimator \(\widehat\psi\) in the control-zero one-arm source experiment on \(d_{\mathrm{rad}}\) cells admits a source law \(P\) such that \[\frac{a_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}}{2} \left\{ \frac1n+\frac{d_{\mathrm{rad}}^2}{n^2\log^2 n} \right\} \le E_{P^{\otimes n}} \left[ \{\widehat\psi-\psi(P)\}^2 \right].\] The same constants also give, for every \(M,\sigma\in\mathbb R\), \[c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}M^2\sigma^2 \min\left\{1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right\} \le \left(\frac{M\sigma}{2}\right)^2 \frac{a_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}}{2} \left\{ \frac1n+ \frac{d_{\mathrm{rad}}^2}{n^2\log^2 n} \right\}.\] Indeed, the cutoff gives \(n\ge3\) and \(2/b_{0,\epsilon}\le n\). Hence \[1\le b_{0,\epsilon}n\log n=2b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}n\log n, \qquad 1\le b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}n\log(en).\] The first inequality controls the \(\max\{1,\cdot\}\) guard in the source-range bound; together with \(\log(en)\le2\log n\) for \(n\ge3\), it yields \(d_{\mathrm{rad}}\le b_{0,\epsilon}n\log n\le b_{\epsilon}n\log n\). The second inequality is the range input for Lemma 16 with parameter \(b_{0,\epsilon}\), which gives the displayed transport scale. The all-sample definition of \(d_{\mathrm{rad}}\) is retained below; the cutoff \(N_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad},0}\le n\) is invoked for the source-range, source-risk, and large-sample transport-scale conclusions in this step.

  2. Use Lemma 15 to choose \[b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}>0, \qquad c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}>0, \qquad N_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}\in\mathbb N\] and, for each \(n,d\ge1\), a source lower-risk level \(L_{\mathrm{ex}}(n,d)\) for the exact-homogeneity capped alphabet \[d_{\mathrm{ex}} := \min\left\{ d,\max\left\{1,\left\lfloor b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}n^2\right\rfloor\right\} \right\}.\] Thus \(0<d_{\mathrm{ex}}\le d\), the exact source estimator-wise lower bound holds at level \(L_{\mathrm{ex}}(n,d)\), and \[c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}M^2 \left\{ \frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right) \right\} \le M^2L_{\mathrm{ex}}(n,d)\] for all \(M\). Independently, Lemma 2 gives a constant \(c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{base}}>0\) such that, for every \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\), \[c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{base}}M^2 \left\{ \frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right) \right\} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\]

  3. Let \[N_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}:=\max\{N_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad},0},1\}, \qquad c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{small}}:=\frac{1}{800N_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}},\] \[c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{tr}} := \min\{c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}},c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{small}},c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}\}, \qquad c_{\epsilon} := \min\left\{\frac{\min\{c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{base}},c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{tr}}\}}{2},1\right\}.\] Then \(0<c_{\epsilon}\le1\), \(c_{\epsilon}\le c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{tr}}\), \(c_{\epsilon}\le c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}\), \(c_{\epsilon}\le c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{small}}\), \(c_{\epsilon}\le c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}\), and \(0<b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}\).

  4. Now fix \(n,d\ge1\), \(M\ge1\), and \(0\le\sigma\le2\). Use the two capped alphabets \(d_{\mathrm{rad}}\) and \(d_{\mathrm{ex}}\) defined above for this \((n,d)\), and embed each capped alphabet into \(\{1,\ldots,d\}\) by the first-coordinate padding map. The radial source family is the full control-zero binary one-arm class on \(d_{\mathrm{rad}}\) cells, and the exact source family is the full exact-homogeneity binary class on \(d_{\mathrm{ex}}\) cells. For each source law, take as full-data coupling the independent binary full-data lift: conditionally on the observed binary law, the missing potential response is drawn from the corresponding binary arm-cell outcome law. These choices preserve the source full-data margins for the radial and exact source families.

    For a radial source law \(P\), the independent coupling has treated potential margin \[E_P[B(1)\mid X=k]=E_P[B\mid A=1,X=k],\] so the source functional defined in Step 1 is equivalently \[\psi(P)=\sum_{k=1}^{d_{\mathrm{rad}}}p_k(P)\,E_P[B(1)\mid X=k].\] Set \[\lambda:=\frac{\sigma}{2},\] and let the channel be \[P\{B'(a)=1\mid B(a)\} = \frac12+\lambda\left(B(a)-\frac12\right), \qquad Y(a):=M\left(B'(a)-\frac12\right).\] Since \(0\le\sigma\le2\), the two channel probabilities are \(1/2-\sigma/4\) and \(1/2+\sigma/4\), and both lie in \([0,1]\). The radial full law is the common two-potential-outcome Bernoulli channel applied to the independent source coupling and then scaled by \(b\mapsto M(b-1/2)\); the exact full law is the deterministic affine image \(Y(a)=M(B(a)-1/2)\) of the exact independent source coupling.

    Padding preserves cell masses and propensities on the image and assigns zero mass outside it. The affine full-data construction supplies consistency and conditional exchangeability; inherited propensities give overlap. Since \(Y(a)\in[-M/2,M/2]\) and \(M\ge1\), the conditional mean normalization and second central moment requirements in Definition 1 hold. On every positive-mass padded radial source cell, \[E[Y(1)\mid X=k] = M\frac{\sigma}{2} \left(E_P[B(1)\mid X=k]-\frac12\right), \qquad E[Y(0)\mid X=k] = -M\frac{\sigma}{4},\] and therefore \[\tau_k = M\frac{\sigma}{2}E_P[B(1)\mid X=k], \qquad \tau = M\frac{\sigma}{2}\psi(P).\] Thus \[|\tau_k-\tau| \le M\frac{\sigma}{2} \le \sigma M,\] so every radial embedded law belongs to \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\). Moreover, for all radial source laws \(P_0,P_1\), writing \(\tau(P_j)\) for the ATE of the embedded law, \[\tau(P_1)-\tau(P_0) = M\frac{\sigma}{2}\{\psi(P_1)-\psi(P_0)\}.\] The exact deterministic affine embedding has target slope \(M\) for the exact-homogeneity source functional.

  5. Let \(\widehat\tau\) be any estimator taking values in \([-M,M]\). The observed radial channel is the common Markov kernel that keeps the padded cell and treatment arm, applies the Bernoulli contraction to the observed binary response, and scales the result to \(M(B'-1/2)\). For all radial source laws \(P_0,P_1\), this common kernel gives the product-sample data-processing bound \[\operatorname{TV}\!\left(Q_{\mathrm{rad},P_0}^{(n)},Q_{\mathrm{rad},P_1}^{(n)}\right) \le \operatorname{TV}\!\left(P_0^{\otimes n},P_1^{\otimes n}\right),\] where \(Q_{\mathrm{rad},P}^{(n)}\) is the observed product law after the radial embedding.

    If \(\sigma=0\), the fixed-sample parametric radial source bound supplies a source law \(P\), and the radial lower bound asserted in the transfer certificate has left side zero; nonnegativity of squared error gives the required risk inequality. Suppose \(\sigma>0\). If \(N_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad},0}\le n\), compose \(\widehat\tau\) with the product channel and set \[\widehat\psi_{\mathrm{rad}}(Z) := \frac{ E\{\widehat\tau(W)\mid Z\} }{ M\sigma/2 },\] where \(Z\) is the binary source sample and \(W\) is the channel output. Since \(M\ge1\) and \(\sigma>0\), the divisor is positive, and the boundedness of \(\widehat\tau\) makes \(\widehat\psi_{\mathrm{rad}}\) uniformly bounded. Jensen’s inequality gives, for each source law \(P\), \[\left(\frac{M\sigma}{2}\right)^2 E_{P^{\otimes n}} \left[ \{\widehat\psi_{\mathrm{rad}}-\psi(P)\}^2 \right] \le E_{Q_{\mathrm{rad},P}^{(n)}} \left[ \{\widehat\tau-\tau(P)\}^2 \right].\] The source lower bound from Step 1 and the radial scale inequality then give some radial source law \(P\) such that \[c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{tr}}M^2\sigma^2 \min\left\{1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right\} \le E_{Q_{\mathrm{rad},P}^{(n)}} \left[ \{\widehat\tau-\tau(P)\}^2 \right].\] If \(N_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad},0}>n\), use the same all-sample capped alphabet \(d_{\mathrm{rad}}\) and the one-arm parametric source lower bound at level \(1/(200n)\). Since \(1\le n\le N_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}\), Lemma 17 gives \[c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{small}}M^2\sigma^2 \min\left\{1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right\} \le \left(\frac{M\sigma}{2}\right)^2\frac{1}{200n},\] and \(c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{tr}}\le c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{small}}\) yields the same estimator-wise radial bound. Since \(c_{\epsilon}\le c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{tr}}\), the radial transfer certificate also holds with \(c_{\epsilon}\), using the cap, source family, padding map, embedding, independent full-data coupling, and channel fixed above.

  6. The deterministic exact embedding is handled analogously. For any bounded ambient estimator, compose it with the exact padding-and-scaling map and divide by \(M\). Since \(M\ge1\), the affine slope is positive. The exact source level \(L_{\mathrm{ex}}(n,d)\) and \(c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{tr}}\le c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{ex}}\) give, for every \(\widehat\tau\), some exact source law whose embedded risk is at least \[c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{tr}}M^2 \left\{ \frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right) \right\}.\] This supplies the exact half of the common transfer certificate; the radial half was established in Step 5.

  7. Because every radial embedded law belongs to \(\mathcal P_{d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}\), Step 5 implies \[c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{tr}}M^2\sigma^2 \min\left\{1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right\} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\] Together with the base lower bound from Step 2, put \[\mathfrak A_{n,d}:= \frac1n+\min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right), \qquad \mathfrak R_{n,d,\sigma}:= \sigma^2\min\left\{1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right\}.\] Then \[c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{base}}M^2\mathfrak A_{n,d} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}, \qquad c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{tr}}M^2\mathfrak R_{n,d,\sigma} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\] Since \(\mathfrak A_{n,d}\) and \(\mathfrak R_{n,d,\sigma}\) are nonnegative, \[\frac{\min\{c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{base}},c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{tr}}\}}{2} M^2(\mathfrak A_{n,d}+\mathfrak R_{n,d,\sigma}) \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\] Finally \(c_{\epsilon}\le \min\{c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{base}},c_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{tr}}\}/2\), so \[c_{\epsilon}M^2 \left\{ \frac1n+ \min\left(1,\frac d{n^2}\right) + \sigma^2\min\left(1,\frac{d^2}{n^2\log^2(en)}\right) \right\} \le \mathsf R_{n,d,\epsilon,M,\sigma}.\] The radial certificate asserted in the theorem is the package from Steps 4 and 5: the same \(b_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{rad}}\), capped alphabet \(d_{\mathrm{rad}}\), source family, first-coordinate padding map, radial embedding, independent full-data coupling, common Bernoulli channel, product data-processing inequality, pairwise target identity, and estimator-wise radial lower bound.

∎
